Updates in develop (50 commits)

Главная / streamlit/streamlit / Updates in develop (50 commits)
Проект: streamlit/streamlit
Тип обновления: UpdateType.COMMIT_BATCH
Дата: 2026-07-24 22:25 UTC
Источник: https://github.com/streamlit/streamlit/commits/develop

Суммаризация (на русском)

Это обновление представляет отложенную загрузку для `st.dataframe` и фоновое обновление для `st.cache_data` и `st.cache_resource`, что значительно повышает производительность при работе с большими наборами данных и кэшированием. Основные исправления включают устранение перезаписи строк в `st.data_editor`, восстановление нечеткого поиска в `st.selectbox` и улучшение отображения `st.popover` в боковых панелях и диалоговых окнах. Пользовательский опыт дополнительно улучшен благодаря поддержке вставки значений в `st.time_input` и новому параметру `icon` для `st.metric`, а также важным обновлениям зависимостей, связанным с безопасностью и обслуживанием.

Исходный текст

Configure [here](https://www.cursor.com/dashboard/bugbot).</sup> <!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY --> Signed-off-by: dependabot[bot] <support@github.com> Co-authored-by: dependabot[bot] <49699333+dependabot[bot]@users.noreply.github.com> - [fix] Prevent st.data_editor row additions overwriting rows (#16144) ## Describe your changes `st.data_editor` row additions could silently **overwrite an existing row**. Since the editor's widget state is client-controlled, an "added" row carrying an explicit index value that already exists in the DataFrame (for non-`RangeIndex` indices) was forwarded to `df.loc[label] = ...`, whose assignment semantics replaced the matching row instead of appending a new one. - Reject added rows whose explicit index collides with an existing index label; a warning is logged and the row is skipped. Other additions in the same batch are unaffected. - Delete-then-re-add of the same index label still works, because deletions are applied before additions. ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) - Internal reference: SNOW-3715994 ## Testing Plan - Unit Tests (Python): `lib/tests/streamlit/elements/data_editor_test.py` - `test_apply_row_additions_existing_index_is_skipped` — a colliding index does not overwrite the existing row. - `test_apply_row_additions_skips_only_duplicate_in_batch` — only the duplicate is skipped; unique additions still apply. - `test_apply_dataframe_edits_delete_then_re_add_same_index` — re-adding a just-deleted index label succeeds. Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com) - Support pasting values in `TimeInput` (#16096) Adds paste support to `st.time_input` (now React Aria-based). Users can paste time values directly into the widget using several formats: - **Full time with colon**: HH:MM (e.g., 14:30) - **Bare digits**: HHMM or HMM (e.g., 0830, 930) - **Partial digits (1–2)**: pasted into the currently focused hour or minute segment Out-of-range or unparseable input shows an inline validation error - same as what number input uses (red border + error icon with tooltip) without committing the value. The prior valid value is restored on: - Arrow key press (reverts display) - Next valid paste or typed change - Clear button click - External value update (session state / form clear) **Accessibility**: The error state is surfaced to screen readers via aria-invalid, `aria-describedby`, and a visually-hidden `role="alert"` element — matching the pattern used by `st.number_input`. - Remove `Baseweb` from `TimeInput` (#15647) Replaces the BaseWeb `TimePicker` (a generated dropdown of time slots) with React Aria Components' `TimeField` for `st.time_input`, continuing BaseWeb removal effort. - [feature] Add background refresh for st.cache_data and st.cache_resource (#16057) ## Describe your changes Adds a `refresh_mode` parameter (`"foreground"` default, or `"background"`) to `st.cache_data` and `st.cache_resource`, implementing bounded stale-while-revalidate. - **`"background"`**: within `[ttl, 2*ttl)` a stale entry is served immediately (non-blocking, no spinner) while a single deduplicated refresh runs on a shared bounded thread pool; past `2*ttl` the entry is evicted and the next access blocks + recomputes (identical to foreground). Adds zero extra compute over foreground. - Underlying cache TTL is set to `2*ttl`, with per-entry freshness tracked via a `stored_at` timestamp on `CachedResult` (survives the `cache_data` pickle round-trip). - Refreshes dedup via the existing per-key compute lock, discard on orphan/detach/`.clear()`/generation-change, apply a per-key failure cooldown, and degrade gracefully to foreground-at-hard-expiry when thread creation is unavailable. - `cache_resource`: replaced resources fire `on_release` (via `safe_del`); orphaned/discarded refresh results are released so nothing leaks; `validate` still gates stale serving. - Background mode disables cached `st.*` replay on hits (warns once if the function issues display commands) and is context-free during refresh (documented in the docstrings). - Validation: `refresh_mode="background"` requires a `ttl` and is incompatible with `persist` for `cache_data` (both raise `StreamlitAPIException`). Spec: `specs/2026-04-08-cache-background-refresh/product-spec.md` (merged in #14690). Builds on the internal `TTLCache` from #16014. > Note: PR #15734 already implements this same spec from a parallel branch. This is an independent implementation opened as a draft alternative for comparison. ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) - Closes #5871 - Partly addresses #11050 ## Testing Plan - [x] Unit Tests (Python): `common_cache_test.py` (parametrized background-refresh behavior over both decorators), `cache_data_api_test.py` / `cache_resource_api_test.py` (validation, hard-TTL, `stored_at`, release, validate-gating, session discard), `cache_background_refresh_test.py` (executor saturation/degradation/slot release), typing assertions in `typing/cache_types.py`. - [x] E2E Tests: `e2e_playwright/st_cache_background_refresh_test.py` (stale-while-revalidate scenario). Configure [here](https://www.cursor.com/dashboard/bugbot).</sup> <!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --------- Signed-off-by: dependabot[bot] <support@github.com> Co-authored-by: dependabot[bot] <49699333+dependabot[bot]@users.noreply.github.com> Co-authored-by: Cursor Agent <cursoragent@cursor.com> - [fix] TypeError in run_every fragments with Sentry threading (#16140) ## Describe your changes `@st.fragment(run_every=...)` raises `TypeError: Timer.run() takes 1 positional argument but 2 were given` when Sentry's `ThreadingIntegration` is enabled. The thread-state wrapper installed by `add_script_run_ctx` captures the thread's **already-bound** `run()` as `original_run`. Sentry treats the replacement `run` as an unbound function and re-invokes it with the thread instance as an extra positional argument. The previous fix (#15376) forwarded `*args`/`**kwargs` to `original_run`, so that spurious `self` was passed to the bound method, causing the double-argument `TypeError`. The wrapper still accepts extra arguments (so third-party wrappers can call it), but no longer forwards them — `Thread.run()`/`Timer.run()` take no positional arguments, so the extra argument is dropped. ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) - Closes #16139 ## Testing Plan - Unit test (`test_run_wrapper_tolerates_extra_positional_arg_from_thread_wrapper`) mimics the Sentry calling pattern against a no-arg `run()`; it reproduces the exact `TypeError` without the fix and passes with it. This replaces the prior test that used a contrived `run(*args)` subclass which masked the bug. <!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --- > [!NOTE] > **Low Risk** > Narrow change to thread `run` wrapping in `add_script_run_ctx`; behavior for normal `Thread.start()` is unchanged and covered by an updated regression test. > > **Overview** > Fixes **`TypeError: Timer.run() takes 1 positional argument but 2 were given`** when **`@st.fragment(run_every=...)`** runs alongside **Sentry's `ThreadingIntegration`**. > > `add_script_run_ctx` wraps a child thread's **`run`** so **`FragmentThreadState`** is initialized before the real **`run`**. The captured **`original_run`** is already bound and takes no arguments. A prior fix forwarded **`*args`/`**kwargs`** into **`original_run`**, so when Sentry re-invoked the wrapper with the thread instance as an extra positional arg, that arg was passed through and broke standard **`Thread`/`Timer.run()`**. > > The wrapper still accepts arbitrary positional/keyword args for third-party hooks but **drops** them and always calls **`original_run()`** with no arguments. The regression test now uses a no-arg **`run()`** and invokes **`t.run(t)`** to match Sentry's calling pattern (replacing a contrived **`run(*args)`** subclass that hid the bug). > > <sup>Reviewed by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/bugbot) for commit 4bfd5890ab8fd5f03f384700870a2078002d757b. Configure [here](https://www.cursor.com/dashboard/bugbot).</sup> <!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --------- Co-authored-by: lawilby <laura.wilby+oss@snowflake.com> - [fix] Commit open data_editor cell edit on outside click (#16132) ## Describe your changes Editing a `st.data_editor` cell and then clicking an element outside the grid (e.g. an `st.button`) discarded the in-progress edit: the outside click triggered a rerun before the 150ms-debounced edit sync had run, so the pre-edit value was sent to the backend. - Adds a `flush()` control to the `useDebouncedCallback` hook that runs a pending callback immediately and cancels its timer. - Wires glide-data-grid's `isOutsideClick`/`onFinishedEditing` in the data editor to flush the pending edit synchronously on outside clicks. All other close paths (Enter/Tab/Escape, clicks inside the grid) keep the normal debounce, so rapid edits are still batched. ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) - Closes https://github.com/streamlit/streamlit/issues/7868 ## Testing Plan - Unit Tests (JS): `useDebouncedCallback.test.ts` covers the new `flush` behavior; `DataFrame.test.tsx` covers flushing on outside clicks vs. staying debounced on other close paths. - E2E Tests: `st_data_editor_editing_test.py::test_clicking_button_commits_open_cell_editor` opens a cell editor, edits it, clicks a button, and asserts the edited value is committed. Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com) --------- Co-authored-by: Cursor <cursoragent@cursor.com> - Fix st.selectbox fuzzy search only matches contiguous substrings since 1.59.0 (#16009) ## Describe your changes Fixes the 1.59.0 regression where `st.selectbox` fuzzy search only matched contiguous substrings. Since the BaseWeb → react-aria `ComboBox` migration (#15438), RAC applied its own default `contains` text filter on top of Streamlit's `fuzzyFilterSelectOptions` result, so the visible list was the intersection (substring-only). Streamlit's fuzzy matcher was still correct in isolation. The fix passes a no-op `defaultFilter` to `<ComboBox>` so Streamlit's own filter (fuzzy / contains / prefix / none) is authoritative and the already-filtered `displayOptions` reach the ListBox unchanged. Per the [React Aria docs](https://react-spectrum.adobe.com/react-aria/ComboBox.html#custom-filtering), `defaultFilter` is the documented way to override the built-in filter. Restores 1.58.0 behavior: | Type | Dropdown | | --- | --- | | `ape` | `Grape`, `Apple` | | `aple` | `Apple` | | `rp` | `Grape` | ## Screenshot or video (only for visual changes) Screenshots below are hosted from a separate `cursor/pr-16003-assets-152a` branch on this fork (so they don't clutter the PR diff). ### Before (bug — 1.59.0 through current) | Query | Result | | --- | --- | | `ape` — only `Grape`, missing `Apple` | ![Before: typing ape shows only Grape](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/LuC-9/streamlit/cursor/pr-16003-assets-152a/bug_query_ape.png) | | `aple` — "No results" | ![Before: typing aple shows No results](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/LuC-9/streamlit/cursor/pr-16003-assets-152a/bug_query_aple.png) | | `rp` — "No results" | ![Before: typing rp shows No results](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/LuC-9/streamlit/cursor/pr-16003-assets-152a/bug_query_rp.png) | ### After (fix) | Query | Result | | --- | --- | | `ape` — `Grape`, `Apple` | ![After: typing ape shows Grape and Apple](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/LuC-9/streamlit/cursor/pr-16003-assets-152a/fix_query_ape.png) | | `aple` — `Apple` | ![After: typing aple shows Apple](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/LuC-9/streamlit/cursor/pr-16003-assets-152a/fix_query_aple.png) | | `rp` — `Grape` | ![After: typing rp shows Grape](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/LuC-9/streamlit/cursor/pr-16003-assets-152a/fix_query_rp.png) | ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) Fixes #16003. Adds an optional `icon: str | None = None` keyword-only parameter to `st.metric` that renders a plain icon inline before the metric label. Mirrors the `icon` convention already used by `st.button` and alert elements (`st.info`, `st.warning`, etc.). ```python st.metric("Temperature", "70 °F", "1.2 °F", icon=":material/thermostat:") ``` Accepts the same values as other `icon` params: - A single-character emoji (e.g. `"🔥"`) - A Material Symbols shortcode (e.g. `":material/thermostat:"`) - `"spinner"` ## Design decisions - **Placement**: inline before the label text, on the same row as the label. - **Styling**: plain — no colored badge or background (matches how alert icons render). - **Interactions**: same rendering regardless of `border`, `chart_data`, or `delta`. - **Validation**: reuses `streamlit.string_util.validate_icon_or_emoji`. `None` maps to the proto3 zero-default `""` so the field is written unconditionally. - **Kwarg placement**: `icon` sits immediately after `help` (keeps label-adjacent kwargs grouped, matches `st.button` ordering). - **Grid layout**: the label markdown is wrapped in a new `StyledMetricLabelRow` inline-flex row so the outer grid (`auto 1fr` for label + help icon) is preserved and the help tooltip icon stays in the second grid cell. ## Test plan - [x] Python unit tests: default (`""`), each valid icon variant (parameterized), and invalid-icon `StreamlitAPIException` (`lib/tests/streamlit/elements/metric_test.py`). - [x] Python typing tests: `assert_type` coverage for all `icon` variants + combined-parameters case (`lib/tests/streamlit/typing/metric_types.py`). - [x] Frontend unit tests: renders when provided, does not render when omitted, does not render when empty string (`Metric.test.tsx`). - [x] E2E: DOM assertion + `st_metric-with_icon` snapshot; Fixes CVE <a href="https://github.com/immutable-js/immutable-js/security/advisories/GHSA-xvcm-6775-5m9r">https://github.com/immutable-js/immutable-js/security/advisories/GHSA-xvcm-6775-5m9r</a></li> </ul> <h2>5.1.7</h2> <ul> <li>fix(Repeat): lastIndexOf returned size instead of size - 1 by <a href="https://github.com/chatman-media"><code>@​chatman-media</code></a> in <a href="https://redirect.github.com/immutable-js/immutable-js/pull/2227">immutable-js/immutable-js#2227</a>. Fixes CVE <a href="https://github.com/immutable-js/immutable-js/security/advisories/GHSA-wf6x-7x77-mvgw">CVE-2026-29063 </a></li> <li>fix(IndexedCollection): <code>has(index)</code> on a lazy <code>Seq</code> of unknown size now checks index existence instead of searching for a value equal to the index <a href="https://redirect.github.com/immutable-js/immutable-js/pull/2203">#2203</a></li> <li>[TypeScript]: <code>reduce</code>/<code>reduceRight</code> without an initial value now infer the result type from the collection's values when the reducer returns a value (e.g. <code>list.reduce((a, b) =&gt; a + b)</code> infers <code>number</code>), matching <code>Array#reduce</code>. Previously an explicit type argument was required. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/immutable-js/immutable-js/pull/2205">#2205</a></li> </ul> <h2>5.1.6</h2> <ul> <li>fix(reverseFactory): read It was previously deprecated in favor of the `width` parameter (`use_column_width="always"`/`True` → `width="stretch"`, `use_column_width="auto"`/`"never"`/`False` → `width="content"`). - Remove the related type alias, docstring section, and the mutual-exclusivity check between `use_container_width` and `use_column_width`. - Remove associated unit tests, typing-test assertions (replaced with a negative `call-arg` assertion), e2e test coverage, and obsolete snapshots. ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) ## Testing Plan - [x] Unit Tests (Python) - [x] E2E Tests (updated `st_image_test.py`) Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com) --------- Co-authored-by: github-actions[bot] <41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com> Co-authored-by: Streamlit Bot <core+streamlitbot-github@streamlit.io> - [feat] Add lazy loading for `st.dataframe` (#15756) ## Describe your changes Implements **Phase 1 + Phase 2** of [Lazy Loading for `st.dataframe`](https://github.com/streamlit/streamlit/blob/9d53518bdc3c52ff393450deaea12461a9009962/specs/2026-05-07-dataframe-lazy-load/product-spec.md) (Polars `LazyFrame` native adapter; the Snowpark adapter is deferred to a follow-up). Adds a tri-state `lazy: bool | None = None` parameter to `st.dataframe`. In lazy mode the frontend requests Arrow row chunks from the backend on demand (over the existing non-rerun `BackendOperationRequest`/`BackendOperationResponse` transport) instead of receiving the full dataset, so very large datasets can be browsed without freezing the browser. **Backend** - Internal `DataframeSource` protocol + in-memory pandas/Polars/PyArrow sources (`lib/streamlit/dataframe/lazy_df_source.py`). - Phase 2 native adapter: Polars `LazyFrame` — `lib/streamlit/dataframe/lazy_df_adapters.py`. (The Snowpark adapter is deferred to a follow-up.) - Session-scoped `DataframeSourceManager` (unguessable source ids, generation + session validation, `MAX_CHUNK_ROWS` cap, rerun/fragment/session cleanup mirroring the media-file manager) and a `DataframeChunkHandler` on the backend-operation dispatcher. - `st.dataframe` resolves delivery mode and marshals `lazy_data` (`lib/streamlit/elements/arrow.py`). **Frontend** - `LazyDataframeCache`, `useLazyDataLoader` (debounced chunk requests, native loading cells, stale-generation handling, FIFO eviction), and `useLazyColumnSort` (server-side sort state), integrated into `DataFrame.tsx` (both eager and lazy hooks always run per the Rules of Hooks). Search, CSV export, select-all, and selections are disabled for lazy sources; sorting is gated on the source `sortable` capability. **Resolution behavior** - `lazy=None`: auto-lazy for in-memory pandas/Polars dataframes above 150,000 rows, and for supported unevaluated sources (Polars `LazyFrame`) above the 10,000-row capped-preview threshold; otherwise eager. - `lazy=True`: native adapter when available, else convert a supported eager input to an in-memory pandas source; inputs <= 1,000 rows stay eager. - `lazy=False`: always eager. - Incompatible `lazy=True` (pandas `Styler`, `on_select`) raises `StreamlitAPIException`. **Proto:** `LazyDataframe`, `SortState`, and dataframe chunk request/response payloads. ### Deliberate divergences from the tech spec - The Snowpark native adapter is deferred to a follow-up; this PR ships the Polars `LazyFrame` native adapter. Resolution for the `LazyFrame` source follows the spec (auto-lazy above the capped-preview threshold for `lazy=None`, opt-in for `lazy=True`). - In-memory sources materialize a single Arrow table and slice/sort it (stable schema across chunks); server memory is unchanged from eager. - `useDataLoader` is not split; lazy loading is a parallel hook selected by `isLazy`. ### Notes - Auto-lazy `lazy=None` tables (in-memory > 150k rows, or a native `LazyFrame` > 10k rows) disable the toolbar **search** affordance for those tables (previously shown). This is an intentional, contained behavior change; opt out with `lazy=False`. - Resolving a native `LazyFrame` under `lazy=None` reads its row count to decide auto-lazy, which triggers a Polars `count()`/scan on each rerun. This matches the product spec's auto-lazy-above-threshold behavior; use `lazy=False` to keep the capped-preview path. - The new e2e visual snapshot (`st_dataframe_lazy-initial`) needs Linux/CI generation. ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) N/A ## Testing Plan - [x] Unit Tests (Python): source resolution matrix, source manager (validation/cleanup), chunk handler, and `st.dataframe` lazy marshalling. - [x] Unit Tests (JS): `LazyDataframeCache`, `useLazyDataLoader`, `useLazyColumnSort`, `useDataFrameCapabilities` lazy gating, and `BackendOperationClient.requestDataframeChunk`. - [x] Typing test for the `lazy` parameter. - [x] E2E tests (`st_dataframe_lazy_test.py`): scroll loads chunks without a rerun, server-side sorting without a rerun, search/CSV hidden for lazy, small lazy stays eager. - [ ] External e2e coverage for proxied websocket chunk transport (recommended follow-up). Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com) --------- Co-authored-by: github-actions[bot] <41898282+github-actions[bot]@users.noreply.github.com> Co-authored-by: Streamlit Bot <core+streamlitbot-github@streamlit.io> - [chore] Add typing tests for vega chart commands (#16084) ## Describe your changes Adds typing tests for the vega/altair chart commands, which were previously uncovered in `lib/tests/streamlit/typing/`. - Covers `st.line_chart`, `st.area_chart`, `st.bar_chart`, and `st.scatter_chart` (return `DeltaGenerator`). - Covers the overloaded return types of `st.altair_chart` and `st.vega_lite_chart` (`DeltaGenerator` vs `VegaLiteState` depending on `on_select`). ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) ## Testing Plan - `lib/tests/streamlit/typing/vega_charts_types.py` — mypy-checked `assert_type` tests for the vega chart commands' parameters and return types (including negative `# type: ignore` cases for invalid arguments). `st.rerun(target=...)` reruns it from a widget callback, wherever the triggering widget lives — no element handles, state classes, or new paradigm - Callback-only restriction keeps execution deterministic, makes rerun cycles structurally impossible, and covers the dashboard-filter / cross-fragment use cases users ask for - Documents alternatives considered (call-time key, declarative `depends_on` graph) and leaves relaxing the callback restriction as the open question Test App Here: https://event-scoped-fragment-updates.streamlit.app/ Prototype Branch Here: https://github.com/streamlit/streamlit/pull/15794 ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) These are all related, but not necessarily all solved by this spec. - #10603 — Rerun fragment from anywhere (25 thumbs-up) - #12799 — Selective rerun + execution control - #10045 — Fragment-to-fragment communication - #12395 — Easier cross-filtering - #12980 — Real-time chart updates without rerunning the script - #9052 — Rerun upon events dispatched by third-party libraries ## Testing Plan - Spec document only, no code changes. --- **Contribution License Agreement** By submitting this pull request you agree that all contributions to this project are made under the Apache 2.0 license. <!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --- > [!NOTE] > **Low Risk** > Spec-only PR with no code or API changes; future implementation would touch execution/rerun behavior but that is not in this diff. > > **Overview** > Adds **`specs/2026-06-23-event-scoped-fragment-reruns/product-spec.md`** — documentation only, no runtime changes. > > The spec defines **event-scoped fragment reruns**: widget callbacks (`on_change` / `on_click`) can refresh only named UI regions instead of the full script, while staying on Streamlit’s rerun model (re-evaluation, not imperative updates). > > **Proposed API:** optional **`@st.fragment(key="...")`** to name a fragment function (unique per run, `"app"` / `"fragment"` reserved). **`st.rerun(scope=...)`** would accept `"app"`, `"fragment"`, a fragment key, or a list of keys; unknown keys fail with `StreamlitAPIException`. Targeted reruns would be **callback-only** for MVP; plain **`st.rerun()` in callbacks** would actually rerun (replacing today’s no-op + warning). A targeted rerun would **replace** the interaction’s default full-app rerun; coalescing would **union** fragment targets unless any request is a full-app rerun, which wins. > > Also records rejected alternatives (`target=`, declarative `depends_on`, call-time `key`), out-of-scope items (server-push, auto dependency inference), docs/metrics checklist, and post-MVP open questions. > > <sup>Reviewed by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/bugbot) for commit e70a967ee1d9b74648bcd0c53e2df2e262c41dae. Updates `@eslint-react/eslint-plugin` from 5.14.10 to 5.16.1 <details> <summary>Release notes</summary> <p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/releases">@​eslint-react/eslint-plugin's releases</a>.</em></p> <blockquote> <h2>v5.16.1 (2026-07-16)</h2> <h2>What's Changed</h2> <h3>🐞 Fixes</h3> <ul> <li><code>react-jsx/no-children-prop</code>: <code>React.createElement</code> calls whose props argument is wrapped in a TypeScript type assertion (e.g. <code>{ children: &quot;x&quot; } as Props</code>) are now reported; computed property keys written as template literals (e.g. <code>{ [</code>children<code>]: &quot;x&quot; }</code>) are now recognized as the <code>children</code> prop; fixed a false positive where a computed identifier key in a <code>createElement</code> props object (e.g. <code>{ [propName]: &quot;Children&quot; }</code>) was treated as the static <code>children</code> prop; the suggestion fix now escapes JSX-sensitive characters (<code>&lt;</code>, <code>&gt;</code>, <code>{</code>, <code>}</code>, <code>&amp;</code>) when moving a string <code>children</code> prop value into element content. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1910">#1910</a>)</li> <li><code>react-jsx/no-children-prop-with-children</code>: same <code>createElement</code> edge-case fixes as <code>no-children-prop</code>. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1910">#1910</a>)</li> <li><code>react-jsx/no-useless-fragment</code>: <code>allowExpressions: false</code> now only flags fragments with a single expression child, matching the option's documented behavior, instead of flagging any fragment inside a JSX element; fragments with spread attributes (e.g. <code>&lt;Fragment {...props}&gt;</code>) are no longer auto-fixed; the auto-fixer now removes leading indentation when collapsing whitespace-only fragments. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1910">#1910</a>)</li> <li><code>react-jsx/no-leaked-dollar</code>: <code>$</code> character references (<code>&amp;[#36](https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/36);</code>, <code>&amp;#x24;</code>) are no longer treated as leaked dollar signs; only a literal <code>$</code> in the source is reported. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1910">#1910</a>)</li> <li><code>react-jsx/no-leaked-semicolon</code>: leaked semicolons followed by spaces or tabs before a newline, including CRLF line endings, are now detected. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1910">#1910</a>)</li> <li><code>react-x/immutability</code>: added <code>useHistory</code> to known navigation hooks, so navigation methods such as <code>.push()</code> are not treated as in-place mutations inside frozen callbacks.</li> </ul> <h3>🏗️ Internal</h3> <ul> <li><code>@eslint-react/ast</code>: added <code>Extract.getStaticPropertyName</code> for resolving static property names from object properties. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1910">#1910</a>)</li> </ul> <p><strong>Full Changelog</strong>: <a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/compare/v5.16.0...v5.16.1">https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/compare/v5.16.0...v5.16.1</a></p> <h2>Attestation</h2> <p><a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/attestations/35539568">https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/attestations/35539568</a></p> <h2>v5.16.0 (2026-07-16)</h2> <h2>What's Changed</h2> <h3>✨ New</h3> <ul> <li> <p><code>@eslint-react/core</code>: added support for hook calls written as tagged template literals. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1909">#1909</a>)</p> <pre lang="tsx"><code>const x = useMotionValue(100); const transform = useMotionTemplate`transform(${x}px)`; // ^^^ tagged template literal hook call </code></pre> </li> <li> <p><code>@eslint-react/jsx</code>: <code>findAttribute</code> now resolves nested spread identifiers and nested object expression spreads. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1908">#1908</a>)</p> </li> </ul> <h3>🏗️ Internal</h3> <ul> <li>Added tests and per-rule changelogs for tagged template literal hook calls. (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1909">#1909</a>)</li> </ul> <p><strong>Full Changelog</strong>: <a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/compare/v5.15.0...v5.16.0">https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/compare/v5.15.0...v5.16.0</a></p> <h2>Attestation</h2> <p><a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/attestations/35506571">https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/attestations/35506571</a></p> <!-- raw HTML omitted --> </blockquote> <p>... Two problems fed the bug: 1. Floating UI's `shift`/`flip` middleware defaulted the boundary to the nearest clipping ancestor. Inside the sidebar that's `StyledSidebarContent` (`overflow: auto`). Since the popover body's `min-width` (20rem) is wider than the sidebar column, `shift` squished it and `flip` pushed it above the viewport once the bottom edge exceeded the sidebar's inner scroll boundary — even though the popover is portalled to `document.body`. 2. When the popover was taller than the space both above and below the trigger, `flip` picked the less-overflowing side but had no way to constrain height, so the popover extended past the viewport top. Fix: when `IsSidebarContext` is `true`, override the `flip`/`shift` boundary to `document.documentElement` (the `<html>` element — `document.body` is `0×0` because `.stApp` uses `position: absolute; inset: 0`), and add a `size` middleware that clamps the popover's `maxHeight` to the available space at the chosen placement. The internal `overflow: auto` on `StyledPopoverBody` then handles scrolling. ## Screenshot or video (only for visual changes) Repro: sidebar with a popover containing ~11 sliders, viewport 1024×600. Before (slider 1 clipped above the viewport top): <img width="1024" height="600" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/61570cba-5893-402b-b8d3-0688d9737ff0" /> After (popover fits within viewport, internal scroll): <img width="1024" height="600" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/202f2e6b-48ff-496a-8dc1-486b08d27806" /> ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) - Closes #9387 ## Testing Plan - Unit Tests (JS): 28/28 Popover vitest pass, no changes needed - E2E Tests: new `test_popover_in_sidebar_stays_within_viewport` asserts the body bounding box stays within the viewport AND extends past the sidebar's right edge (proving it escapes the clipping ancestor) - Manual: verified in `make debug` at 1024×600 that the popover body no longer extends off-screen and scrolls internally when content exceeds available space <!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --- > [!NOTE] > **Low Risk** > Scoped to sidebar popovers via context; main-app popover behavior is unchanged when not in the sidebar. > > **Overview** > Fixes **sidebar `st.popover`** bodies clipping or flipping off-screen when content is taller than the space around the trigger. > > When `IsSidebarContext` is true, positioning now passes **custom Floating UI options** into `useFloatingOverlay`: `flip`/`shift` use **`document.documentElement`** as the boundary (so the portalled popover is limited by the viewport, not the sidebar’s `overflow: auto` ancestor), and an extra **`size`** middleware sets **`maxHeight`** from available space so tall content scrolls inside `StyledPopoverBody` instead of extending past the top of the screen. > > Adds Playwright regression **`test_popover_in_sidebar_stays_within_viewport`** (1024×600) asserting the popover rect stays in the viewport and still extends past the sidebar’s right edge. > > <sup>Reviewed by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/bugbot) for commit c7e7cc18a7500dfcf3a3a2690bd598b73b960176. The popover's own dismiss behavior and any nested overlay (selectbox dropdown, date picker, etc.) were affected the same way. **Root cause:** `@floating-ui/react`'s `FloatingPortal` (used by `st.popover`) creates its host `<div>` directly under `document.body`, which makes it a sibling of the dialog. React Aria's `ModalOverlay` (introduced in the dialog migration) applies `inert` to all siblings of the open dialog to enforce modal focus containment. The popover body's subtree becomes inert and loses hit-testing even though it visually renders on top of the dialog. **Fix:** create a persistent portal host in `PortalProvider` tagged with: - `data-react-aria-top-layer="true"` — opts the subtree out of React Aria's inert sweep - `data-st-overlay-root="true"` — signals to dialog/popover dismissal handlers that interactions inside are "inside the overlay stack" (matches the existing pattern for the dataframe portal) Route `Popover`'s `FloatingPortal` through this host via its stable `id`. Same shape as the existing dataframe overlay host. Supersedes #16063 (fork PR — reopened from same-repo branch so CI runs with full permissions). ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) - Closes #16005 ## Testing Plan - Unit test: `PortalProvider.test.tsx` verifies both portal hosts are mounted with the correct top-layer + overlay-root attributes and that the floating overlay host is a direct child of `document.body` (sibling of the dialog). - E2E test: `test_dialog_allows_interacting_with_widget_in_popover` opens a dialog with a popover containing a selectbox, picks an option, and verifies (a) the widget is interactable, (b) the picked value renders, (c) the enclosing dialog stays open. - Manual: reproduced with the exact snippet from #16005 before the fix (selectbox unclickable, `elementFromPoint` returns the dialog); confirmed working after. <!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --- > [!NOTE] > **Medium Risk** > Touches global overlay stacking and React Aria top-layer behavior for all popovers; risk is mitigated by matching an existing portal pattern and targeted E2E coverage, but could affect dismissal/stacking edge cases. > > **Overview** > Fixes **#16005**: widgets inside an `st.popover` opened from an `st.dialog` were unclickable because React Aria’s dialog overlay marks sibling DOM nodes `inert`, which broke hit-testing on the default `FloatingPortal` host under `document.body`. > > **PortalProvider** now mounts a second body-level host (`stFloatingOverlayPortal`) with `data-react-aria-top-layer` and `data-st-overlay-root`, mirroring the existing dataframe portal pattern. **`st.popover`** routes its `FloatingPortal` through that host so the popover subtree stays interactive and clicks don’t dismiss the dialog. > > Adds **unit** coverage on the new host’s attributes/placement and a **Playwright** regression (dialog → popover → selectbox). > > <sup>Reviewed by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/bugbot) for commit e54e713b8b780b5ab9fcbf7a34d961f98993b683. Bugbot is set up for automated code reviews on this repo. Configure [here](https://www.cursor.com/dashboard/bugbot).</sup> <!-- /CURSOR_SUMMARY --> - [bugfix] Fix inconsistent checkbox margin in horizontal container within column (#13162) (#16060) ## Describe your changes Fixes an inconsistent `margin-top` (and mirrored `margin-bottom`) on checkboxes when they are placed inside an `st.container(horizontal=True)` that is itself inside an `st.columns()` column. The alignment margin applied to the first-of-type / last-of-type checkbox in a TOP or BOTTOM aligned column was using a descendant selector, so it matched *any* first-of-type element container inside the column — including ones nested inside a horizontal container. As a result the first checkbox in each nested horizontal container was getting an extra `0.5rem` of `margin-top` while its siblings were not, producing visibly misaligned rows. Scope the selector to direct children of the column's own `stVerticalBlock` so the margin only applies to the intended direct-child checkboxes. ## Screenshot or video (only for visual changes) Before <img width="839" height="243" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/058883b3-7659-49fd-9ae9-819e0f6430d9" /> After: <img width="836" height="237" alt="image" src="https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/652b3631-ffef-401d-a860-1bbebf7215ac" /> ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) Closes #13162 ## Testing Plan - **New E2E test**: added `test_column_top_alignment_does_not_leak_into_nested_horizontal_container` in `e2e_playwright/st_columns_test.py`, backed by a new "Vertical alignment - top with nested horizontal container" section in `e2e_playwright/st_columns.py`. The test asserts every checkbox in the nested horizontal container has `margin-top: 0px`. - **Existing E2E tests preserved**: `test_column_vertical_alignment_top` still asserts that a direct-child first checkbox in a TOP-aligned column has `margin-top: 8px`, and `test_column_vertical_alignment_bottom` still asserts the mirrored case — so the intended alignment behavior is not regressed. - **Manual verification**: ran the reproducer from the issue locally and confirmed via `getComputedStyle` that all six checkboxes now report `margin-top: 0px` while the intended direct-child cases still report `margin-top: 8px` / `margin-bottom: 8px`. <!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --- > [!NOTE] > **Low Risk** > CSS-only scoping change for column layout with targeted E2E coverage; no auth, data, or API impact. > > **Overview** > Fixes misaligned checkbox rows when `st.container(horizontal=True)` sits inside a **top**- or **bottom**-aligned `st.columns()` column. > > Column vertical-alignment styles previously used broad descendant selectors, so the first checkbox in a nested horizontal container incorrectly picked up the same `margin-top` / `margin-bottom` meant for direct column widgets. Made with [Cursor](https://cursor.com) - [bugfix] Include decorators in st.echo output (#16068) ## Describe your changes When a decorated function/class was the first statement inside `with st.echo():`, the `@decorator` lines were missing from the echoed source: ```python with st.echo(): @decorator def function(): pass ``` ...rendered just `def function(): / pass`. Adding any statement above the decorated node — even a bare string — happened to "fix" it, because the source range then started at that earlier statement's line. **Root cause:** `ast.FunctionDef.lineno` (and `ClassDef`, `AsyncFunctionDef`) points at the `def`/`class` line — the decorators sit on the lines above. The existing code used `body[0].lineno` as the echo block start, which skipped the decorators. **Fix:** for the first body node, use the min of `node.lineno` and each `decorator_list[i].lineno`. Single decorator, stacked decorators, and non-decorated first statements all take the correct start line. Supersedes #16064 (fork PR — reopened from same-repo branch so CI runs with full permissions). ## GitHub Issue Link (if applicable) - Closes #9252 ## Testing Plan - Unit test: `test_decorated_function_as_first_statement` in `lib/tests/streamlit/elements/echo_test.py` covers both a single-decorator function and a class with two stacked decorators as the first body statement. All 17 existing tests still pass. <!-- CURSOR_SUMMARY --> --- > [!NOTE] > **Low Risk** > Localized display logic in st.echo with a unit test; no auth, data, or runtime execution changes beyond correct source slicing. > > **Overview** > Fixes **`st.echo`** so echoed source includes **`@decorator`** lines when the first statement in the block is a decorated function or class (closes #9252). > > Echo range start no longer uses only `body[0].lineno` (which AST sets on the `def`/`class` line). It now uses the **earliest line** among that node and its `decorator_list`, so single and stacked decorators are shown. Non-decorated first statements behave as before. > > Adds **`test_decorated_function_as_first_statement`** for one-decorator function and two-decorator class cases. > > <sup>Reviewed by [Cursor Bugbot](https://cursor.com/bugbot) for commit 0d2b22fc0804eb53ca3198ce5ab217c713e47495. These versions of CodeQL were discontinued on 1 July 2026 alongside GitHub Enterprise Server 3.16, and will be unsupported by the next minor release of the CodeQL Action. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3956">#3956</a></li> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.1">2.26.1</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4019">#4019</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.37.0 - 08 Jul 2026</h2> <ul> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.0">2.26.0</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3995">#3995</a></li> <li>In addition to the existing input format, the <code>config-file</code> input for the <code>codeql-action/init</code> step will soon support a new <code>[owner/]repo[@ref][:path]</code> format. All components except the repository name are optional. If omitted, <code>owner</code> defaults to the same owner as the repository the analysis is running for, <code>ref</code> to <code>main</code>, and <code>path</code> to <code>.github/codeql-action.yaml</code>. Support for this format ships in this version of the CodeQL Action, but will only be enabled over the coming weeks. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3973">#3973</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.36.3 - 01 Jul 2026</h2> <p>No user facing changes.</p> <h2>4.36.2 - 04 Jun 2026</h2> <ul> <li>Cache CodeQL CLI version information across Actions steps. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3943">#3943</a></li> <li>Reduce requests while waiting for analysis processing by using exponential backoff when polling SARIF processing status. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3937">#3937</a></li> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.25.6">2.25.6</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3948">#3948</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.36.1 - 02 Jun 2026</h2> <p>No user facing changes.</p> <h2>4.36.0 - 22 May 2026</h2> <ul> <li><em>Breaking change</em>: Bump the minimum required CodeQL bundle version to 2.19.4. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3894">#3894</a></li> <li>Add support for SHA-256 Git object IDs. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3893">#3893</a></li> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.25.5">2.25.5</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3926">#3926</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.35.5 - 15 May 2026</h2> <ul> <li>We have improved how the JavaScript bundles for the CodeQL Action are generated to avoid duplication across bundles and reduce the size of the repository by around 70%. This should have no effect on the runtime behaviour of the CodeQL Action. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3899">#3899</a></li> <li>For performance and accuracy reasons, <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/roadmap/issues/1158">improved incremental analysis</a> will now only be enabled on a pull request when diff-informed analysis is also enabled for that run. If diff-informed analysis is unavailable (for example, because the PR diff ranges could not be computed), the action will fall back to a full analysis. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3791">#3791</a></li> <li>If multiple inputs are provided for the GitHub-internal <code>analysis-kinds</code> input, only <code>code-scanning</code> will be enabled. The <code>analysis-kinds</code> input is experimental, for GitHub-internal use only, and may change without notice at any time. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3892">#3892</a></li> <li>Added an experimental change which, when running a Code Scanning analysis for a PR with <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/roadmap/issues/1158">improved incremental analysis</a> enabled, prefers CodeQL CLI versions that have a cached overlay-base database for the configured languages. This speeds up analysis for a repository when there is not yet a cached overlay-base database for the latest CLI version. We expect to roll this change out to everyone in May. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3880">#3880</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.35.4 - 07 May 2026</h2> <ul> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.25.4">2.25.4</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3881">#3881</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.35.3 - 01 May 2026</h2> <!-- raw HTML omitted --> </blockquote> <p>... These versions of CodeQL were discontinued on 1 July 2026 alongside GitHub Enterprise Server 3.16, and will be unsupported by the next minor release of the CodeQL Action. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3956">#3956</a></li> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.1">2.26.1</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/4019">#4019</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.37.0 - 08 Jul 2026</h2> <ul> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.26.0">2.26.0</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3995">#3995</a></li> <li>In addition to the existing input format, the <code>config-file</code> input for the <code>codeql-action/init</code> step will soon support a new <code>[owner/]repo[@ref][:path]</code> format. All components except the repository name are optional. If omitted, <code>owner</code> defaults to the same owner as the repository the analysis is running for, <code>ref</code> to <code>main</code>, and <code>path</code> to <code>.github/codeql-action.yaml</code>. Support for this format ships in this version of the CodeQL Action, but will only be enabled over the coming weeks. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3973">#3973</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.36.3 - 01 Jul 2026</h2> <p>No user facing changes.</p> <h2>4.36.2 - 04 Jun 2026</h2> <ul> <li>Cache CodeQL CLI version information across Actions steps. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3943">#3943</a></li> <li>Reduce requests while waiting for analysis processing by using exponential backoff when polling SARIF processing status. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3937">#3937</a></li> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.25.6">2.25.6</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3948">#3948</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.36.1 - 02 Jun 2026</h2> <p>No user facing changes.</p> <h2>4.36.0 - 22 May 2026</h2> <ul> <li><em>Breaking change</em>: Bump the minimum required CodeQL bundle version to 2.19.4. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3894">#3894</a></li> <li>Add support for SHA-256 Git object IDs. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3893">#3893</a></li> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.25.5">2.25.5</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3926">#3926</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.35.5 - 15 May 2026</h2> <ul> <li>We have improved how the JavaScript bundles for the CodeQL Action are generated to avoid duplication across bundles and reduce the size of the repository by around 70%. This should have no effect on the runtime behaviour of the CodeQL Action. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3899">#3899</a></li> <li>For performance and accuracy reasons, <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/roadmap/issues/1158">improved incremental analysis</a> will now only be enabled on a pull request when diff-informed analysis is also enabled for that run. If diff-informed analysis is unavailable (for example, because the PR diff ranges could not be computed), the action will fall back to a full analysis. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3791">#3791</a></li> <li>If multiple inputs are provided for the GitHub-internal <code>analysis-kinds</code> input, only <code>code-scanning</code> will be enabled. The <code>analysis-kinds</code> input is experimental, for GitHub-internal use only, and may change without notice at any time. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3892">#3892</a></li> <li>Added an experimental change which, when running a Code Scanning analysis for a PR with <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/roadmap/issues/1158">improved incremental analysis</a> enabled, prefers CodeQL CLI versions that have a cached overlay-base database for the configured languages. This speeds up analysis for a repository when there is not yet a cached overlay-base database for the latest CLI version. We expect to roll this change out to everyone in May. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3880">#3880</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.35.4 - 07 May 2026</h2> <ul> <li>Update default CodeQL bundle version to <a href="https://github.com/github/codeql-action/releases/tag/codeql-bundle-v2.25.4">2.25.4</a>. <a href="https://redirect.github.com/github/codeql-action/pull/3881">#3881</a></li> </ul> <h2>4.35.3 - 01 May 2026</h2> <!-- raw HTML omitted --> </blockquote> <p>... (<a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/tree/HEAD/plugins/eslint-plugin/issues/1905">#1905</a>)</li> <li>Rules that match calls by method name no longer treat computed string-literal property access as a static match (e.g. <code>obj[&quot;foo&quot;]()</code> is no longer resolved to <code>&quot;foo&quot;</code>). This avoids relying on the runtime value of computed keys and aligns callee matching across the codebase. Affected rules: <ul> <li><code>react-dom/no-dangerously-set-innerhtml</code></li> <li><code>react-dom/no-find-dom-node</code></li> <li><code>react-dom/no-flush-sync</code></li> <li><code>react-web-api/no-leaked-event-listener</code></li> <li><code>react-web-api/no-leaked-fetch</code></li> <li><code>react-x/globals</code></li> <li><code>react-x/immutability</code></li> </ul> </li> <li><code>core.isJsxLike</code>: no longer treats <code>React['createElement']</code> calls as JSX-like, since the callee is accessed through a computed member expression.</li> </ul> <h3>🏗️ Internal</h3> <ul> <li>Simplified callee name checks across <code>core</code>, <code>react-dom</code>, <code>react-web-api</code>, and <code>react-x</code> rules using <code>Extract.getCalleeName</code>.</li> <li>Bumped <code>@effect/platform</code>, <code>effect</code>, <code>fumadocs-core</code>, <code>fumadocs-mdx</code>, <code>fumadocs-ui</code>, <code>postcss</code>, <code>pnpm</code>, and <code>tsdown</code>.</li> </ul> <p><strong>Full Changelog</strong>: <a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/compare/v5.14.8...v5.14.9">https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/compare/v5.14.8...v5.14.9</a></p> <h2>Attestation</h2> <p><a href="https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/attestations/35295648">https://github.com/Rel1cx/eslint-react/attestations/35295648</a></p> <h2>v5.14.8 (2026-07-14)</h2> <h2>What's Changed</h2> <!-- raw HTML omitted --> </blockquote> <p>...
